Apr 25 Claude responded: The Problem With HowItWorks Weighed naming alternatives for universal mechanics layer Weighed naming alternatives for universal mechanics layer The Problem With HowItWorks It is accurate but the register is wrong. It reads as a casual label rather than a field with a precise assertion type. More importantly it does not signal the zero focalization cognitive mode required — you could write reader experience design or character perception into a field called HowItWorks without feeling the violation. The name should make the mode feel wrong to violate. The content this track holds across all three entity types is the same: the invariant operational truth about this thing, stated as structural fact, independent of any character's knowledge or the story's timeline. The name should work cleanly for earth pony magic, the Stagnation of Harmony, and the Universal Translator without requiring a different mental model for each. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Candidates *Mechanics* The most precise single word. In physics it means the rules governing how a system operates. In game design it means the rules players interact with. Both connotations are correct for your use case. Earth pony magic has mechanics. The Griffonian Republic's economic model has mechanics. The Universal Translator has mechanics. The word signals operational rules rather than historical events or psychological states, which correctly excludes the content that belongs in other tracks. Clean, short, works across all three entity types without forcing. Tooltip: /State the invariant operational rules of this entity — how it actually works, what its inputs and outputs are, what its constraints and failure conditions are. Written in zero focalization: no character's understanding of it, no reader experience design, no narrative deployment. The mechanics are true whether or not any character in the story knows them./ ------------------------------------------------------------------------ *Operating Principles* Slightly more formal than Mechanics, which may be appropriate given that abstract systems like the Stagnation of Harmony or Auftragstaktik doctrine are not mechanical in the engineering sense but do have governing principles that operate consistently. The word Principles signals that this is about invariant rules rather than historical application. The word Operating signals that these rules are active and consequential rather than merely definitional. Tooltip: /Document the governing rules by which this entity operates — what it does, how it does it, what constrains it, what causes it to function or fail. This is the author's complete operational understanding, written from outside time and outside any character's knowledge. Ask: if I needed to verify that a scene using this entity is internally consistent, what would I check against? Write that./ ------------------------------------------------------------------------ *Structural Truth* Already used on connection entities for scene-specific objective content, which creates a naming collision that would cause confusion when scanning the UI. Excluded for that reason despite being otherwise accurate. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ *Invariant Rules* Precise and signals the most important property of this content — that it does not change based on who knows it or when it is revealed. The word Invariant is an engineering and mathematics term meaning a condition that holds regardless of other variables. This correctly distinguishes Layer 1 content from Layer 2 historical record, which is also objective but is time-indexed. The mechanics of earth pony magic are invariant. The history of how Grover III discovered them is not. Tooltip: /Write the rules that govern this entity regardless of time, character knowledge, or narrative position. These are the constraints that your story cannot violate without breaking internal consistency. Neither character perception nor historical context belongs here — only the operational truth that holds from the beginning of the world to the end./ ------------------------------------------------------------------------ *Functional Architecture* Engineering-flavored, which fits your background and fits the three entity types well. Architecture signals that this is about structure rather than surface, and that the structure is designed rather than arbitrary. Functional signals that this describes what the thing does and how rather than what it looks like or what it means thematically. The combination works particularly well for technology entries and abstract systems, and acceptably for world physics. Tooltip: /Document how this entity is built to function — its operating logic, its dependencies, its constraints, its outputs given specific inputs. Written as a structural specification, not a narrative description. A reader of this field should be able to determine whether any scene using this entity is mechanically consistent, without needing any other context./ ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Recommendation *Mechanics* for the track name, with the tooltip from that entry. The single word is the right length for a UI label. It works without forcing across world physics, abstract systems, and technology. It carries the correct connotation of operational rules rather than historical events or psychological states, which does the implicit work of distinguishing it from Track B. And it is the word your engineering instinct would reach for when documenting how a system operates — which means the cognitive mode it implies is already natural to you and will not require conscious calibration each time you use it. You said: What about calling it Ontology? What about calling it Ontology? Apr 25 Claude responded: Ontology Is the Best Term Ontology Is the Best Term It is better than all the candidates proposed, and the reasoning is worth stating precisely so the choice is grounded rather than just intuitive. Ontology in philosophy means the study of what exists and the nature of being — the foundational rules that determine what is real and how real things relate to each other. In Tolkien's own framework, which you have been working within since the first session, he used the word explicitly in "On Fairy-Stories" when discussing the internal consistency requirement of a secondary world. The fictional world must possess what Tolkien called a consistent ontology — rules of being that hold independent of any story told within that world. Your Layer 1 content is precisely this. Earth pony magic scaling with charitostatics is an ontological claim about what earth pony magic fundamentally is and how it operates at the level of the world's physics. The Stagnation of Harmony's mechanisms are ontological claims about how harmony operates as a force in this world. The Universal Translator's cavity magnetron process is an ontological claim about how intent and language relate at the physical level. These are not historical facts, not psychological truths, not narrative architecture decisions. They are claims about the nature of being in this world. The word also does precise work that the engineering candidates did not do. Mechanics, Functional Architecture, and Operating Principles all imply a designed artifact — something built by an agent with a purpose. Ontology has no such implication. Earth pony magic was not designed by anyone. It is a property of reality. Calling the track Ontology correctly signals that this content describes reality rather than a constructed system, even when the entry is a technology that was in fact constructed. The Universal Translator's ontological entry documents what it actually is at the level of physical law — the cavity magnetron process, the magical vector space, the bilingual requirement — not how Rosewing decided to build it. The historical decisions belong in Track B. The physical reality of what was built belongs in Ontology. The term also pairs precisely with your stated framework. You described your project as materialist historicist, grounded in the ontological commitments of the world before any historical events occur. The ontology is what makes the history causally coherent. The charitostatic physics is why the Equestrian Republic is materially superior — not morally preferable by authorial assertion but physically superior by ontological law. Calling the track Ontology makes this relationship explicit every time you open it. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Tooltip /State what this entity fundamentally is and how it operates at the level of this world's physical and structural reality. Written in zero focalization: no character's understanding, no historical context, no narrative deployment. This is the ontological ground truth that exists before the story begins and constrains everything that happens within it. If a scene using this entity violates what is written here, the story's internal consistency is broken./ ------------------------------------------------------------------------ One Refinement Worth Noting For abstract systems — the Stagnation of Harmony as a political-cultural institution, the Griffonian Republic's economic model, Auftragstaktik as a military doctrine — Ontology is still the correct term but the content has a slightly different character than for world physics and technology. An institution's ontological entry documents what it structurally is and how it operates, but the distinction from Track B historical record requires careful discipline. The Stagnation of Harmony's ontological entry documents what the Stagnation is as a structural mechanism — what it does to magical development, how it enforces itself, what its constraints and failure conditions are. How Celestia built it and why belongs in Track B. The tooltip's phrase "exists before the story begins" handles this correctly for abstract systems: the Stagnation's operational mechanics are what they are at the start of TLTT, independent of how they were historically constructed. The ontological entry documents the system as a given of the story's world. Track B documents the causal history that produced it. The distinction holds cleanly if the tooltip is followed precisely. You said: How about if the entity category "World Mechanics" has the note track "Ontology", the entity category "Abstract System" has note track "Axioms", the entity cat… How about if the entity category "World Mechanics" has the note track "Ontology", the entity category "Abstract System" has note track "Axioms", the entity category "Technology" has note track "Mechanics", and the entity category "Organization" has note track "Binding Logic"? Apr 25 Claude responded: Evaluating Each Pairing Evaluating Each Pairing World Mechanics / Ontology *World Mechanics* as a category name is accurate but slightly redundant — mechanics already implies worldness in this context, and the category is sitting alongside other categories whose names do not repeat the word. A cleaner alternative is *World Physics*, which signals that these entries describe invariant physical laws of the secondary world — things that operate the same way regardless of who knows them or when. Physics carries the connotation of discovered rather than designed, which correctly distinguishes this category from Technology. Another candidate is *Natural Law*, which Tolkien's own framework would recognize — these are the rules of subcreation, the fabric of the world before any agent acts on it. World Physics is the stronger term because Physics is a precise disciplinary reference that implies measurability, consistency, and independence from observer, all of which are properties you want this content to have. *Ontology* as the track name is confirmed as the best term for reasons already established. The next candidate is *Substrate* — the underlying reality that everything else rests on — which has engineering precision but loses the philosophical depth that Ontology carries. Ontology wins. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Abstract System / Axioms *Abstract System* is the weakest category name in the set and needs the most reconsideration. The word Abstract is doing too much work and doing it imprecisely. Every entry in the story planner is abstract in some sense. What distinguishes this category from others is not that it is abstract but that it describes something that was *built by agents* — a regime, an economic model, a doctrine, an institution — and that operates as a *set of governing rules* for the entities within it. It is not a physical law like earth pony magic. It is not an organization with membership. It is a framework that shapes behavior without having members. Better category names: *Doctrine* covers military and ideological frameworks precisely — Auftragstaktik, the Stagnation of Harmony as policy, Coltbert's economic model — but fails for economic systems and political constitutions that are not quite doctrines. *Institution* is used in sociology to mean exactly what you mean here — a structured set of rules and norms that govern behavior within a domain — but Institution has overlap with Organization that could cause confusion. *Framework* is clean and broad enough to cover all cases — an economic framework, a political framework, a military doctrine — and correctly signals that these entries describe governing rules rather than physical laws or membership-based groups. It is the strongest alternative. *System* alone, without Abstract, is more precise. The Stagnation of Harmony is a system. The Griffonian Republic's economic model is a system. Auftragstaktik is a system. The word is clean and does not carry the confusion Abstract introduces. The recommendation is *System* as the category name, which pairs cleanly with the other category names in terms of register and length. *Axioms* as the track name for this category is conceptually interesting but slightly misapplied. In mathematics and logic, axioms are foundational assumptions taken as given without proof — the starting conditions from which everything else is derived. A system's axioms would be its foundational assumptions. But what you want to document in this track is not just the starting assumptions but the full operational logic of how the system works — its rules, its enforcement mechanisms, its internal tensions, its failure conditions. Axioms implies only the starting premises, not the operational logic derived from them. Better track names for System entries: *Governing Logic* — signals that this describes the rules by which the system governs behavior within its domain. Clear, precise, non-technical. *Operating Rules* — engineering-flavored, precise about what is being documented, but slightly flat. *Structural Logic* — emphasizes that this is about the system's structure rather than its history or its membership. Strong candidate. *Axioms* is defensible if you reframe it as the foundational principles from which the system's behavior is derived — the Stagnation of Harmony's axiom being "unchecked ambition corrupts, therefore ambition must be constrained." But this would document only the foundational principles, not the full operational mechanics, which means you would need another field for the operational consequences. This splits what should be unified. The recommendation is *Governing Logic* as the track name. It signals zero focalization, it signals operational content rather than historical content, and it correctly scopes to rules rather than events or psychology. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Technology / Mechanics *Technology* as a category name is the most straightforward of the set and requires the least evaluation. It correctly scopes to invented artifacts and engineered systems — the Universal Translator, the Luna Nova Rifle, the Love Harvester, Applejack's Radio. The only meaningful alternative is *Artifact*, which emphasizes that these are made things rather than discovered laws, which is precisely the distinction from World Physics. Artifact is slightly more precise but Technology is more immediately legible and widely understood. Technology stays. *Mechanics* as the track name for Technology entries is correct and well-motivated. The next candidate is *Engineering Principles*, which is more specific about the cognitive mode required but is longer and slightly redundant given that the category is already Technology. *Specifications* is another candidate — in engineering, specifications document exactly what a technology is and how it operates — but Specifications implies a design document for future construction rather than a record of what already exists. Mechanics wins and is confirmed. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Organization / Binding Logic *Organization* as a category name needs clarification against System because the distinction between the two is genuinely subtle and worth establishing precisely before evaluating the names. The distinction is: A *System* has rules and mechanisms but no membership. The Stagnation of Harmony is a system — it shapes behavior through cultural and legal enforcement, but you cannot be a member of the Stagnation of Harmony. It exists as a framework that entities operate within. Its Track B documents how it was constructed. Its Governing Logic track documents how it operates. An *Organization* has membership and a specific binding principle that holds members together. EEEE is an organization — it has members who joined for reasons, who are held together by shared commitments, who act collectively in ways the system does not require. The Changeling Heer is an organization. Star Energy Corporation is an organization. The Wonderbolts are an organization. An organization can be dissolved, can change its membership, can be betrayed from within, can evolve its mission. A system cannot be betrayed — it can only be reformed or dismantled. The further distinction from Character is that an organization has collective agency that is not reducible to any individual member's psychology. EEEE's behavior is not Flowing Current's behavior. The GR military's behavior is not Trimmel's behavior. The organization as an entity has its own Track A psychology — what holds it together — that is distinct from any member's Track A. With that distinction established: *Organization* as the category name is correct but has a slight bureaucratic flavor that undersells the variety of entities it covers. The Temberik tribes are organizations. The Aquileian Cartel is an organization. The Thestrals' parloir network is an organization. These are not all organizations in the corporate sense. *Faction* is a strong alternative — it covers ideological groups, military units, economic collectives, and cultural movements without implying formal structure. It also carries the connotation of a group with interests that may conflict with other factions, which is accurate for how organizations function in your story. *Collective* is another candidate — precise, covers formal and informal groups equally, no corporate connotation. *Organization* is the most immediately legible and works at the UI level without requiring interpretation. The recommendation is to keep *Organization* but define it clearly in the application's onboarding documentation as distinct from System using the membership criterion. *Binding Logic* is a strong track name and deserves careful evaluation before confirming. The content of this track documents what holds the organization together — the shared commitment, the mutual interest, the enforcement mechanism, the mythology that members believe about themselves. EEEE is bound by solidarity and class consciousness. The Changeling Heer is bound by meritocracy under Chrysalis's VOPS system. The Thestrals' parloir network is bound by a shared vocation and theological self-conception. This is genuinely different from Governing Logic in System entries — Governing Logic describes rules that apply to entities within a system, while Binding Logic describes what holds members of an organization to each other. The word Binding is precise — it means the force that prevents the organization from dissolving, the answer to the question "why do these members stay and act collectively?" The word Logic signals that this is structural and operational rather than historical or psychological. Together they produce a term that correctly distinguishes organization- level content from character-level psychology (why does this individual member belong) and from system-level content (what rules govern behavior within this domain). Next candidates for this track: *Cohesion Principles* — emphasizes what holds the organization together rather than its operational rules. Slightly more passive than Binding Logic. *Founding Logic* — risks confusion with Track B historical content about how the organization was founded. Excluded. *Organizational Architecture* — precise but long and slightly redundant given the category name. *Binding Logic* is confirmed as the best term. It is precise, it correctly signals the cognitive mode, and the slight metaphorical quality of Binding works in its favor — an organization's binding logic is what you would need to understand to predict whether the organization holds together under pressure, which is exactly what the story planner needs this content for. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ The Full Evaluated Set Category Recommended Name Track Recommended Name World Mechanics *World Physics* Ontology *Ontology* Abstract System *System* Axioms *Governing Logic* Technology *Technology* Mechanics *Mechanics* Organization *Organization* Binding Logic *Binding Logic* The changes from your proposal are: World Mechanics becomes World Physics for precision; Abstract System becomes System to remove the imprecise Abstract; Axioms becomes Governing Logic to correctly scope to operational rules rather than just foundational premises. The other terms are confirmed. Please evaluate this example and the other questions in order to solidify the 4 (or more) codex entry categories Show more Apr 25 Claude responded: The Naming Problem With World Physics Analyzed ontological categories and mapped content distribution across codex entries Analyzed ontological categories and mapped content distribution across codex entries The Naming Problem With World Physics You are correct that Physics carries a disciplinary connotation that excludes biology, ecology, and behavioral truths. The underlying requirement for this category is a name that signals *invariant truths that exist regardless of whether any character has discovered, named, or understood them* — the foundational reality of the world that all historical events and character psychologies operate within. *World Laws* is the strongest replacement. Laws in the scientific sense means invariant regularities that hold regardless of observer — Newton's laws, Mendel's laws, the laws of thermodynamics. This usage encompasses physics, biology, magical rules, and crucially, game-theoretic realities. The word Laws also signals that these entries constrain the story — the narrative cannot violate them without breaking internal consistency. Short, precise, domain-agnostic. Next candidates: *Fundamental Truths* aligns with your ontology vocabulary but is slightly redundant given the track is already called Ontology. *World Constants* correctly implies these don't change based on who knows them, but Constants sounds mathematical in a limiting way. *Invariants* is technically precise but too jargon-heavy for a UI label. *World Laws* wins. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Where Economics Belongs Your philosophical framing is correct and produces the right answer. Game theory, behavioral economics, and ecological dynamics describe regularities that always existed as features of how rational agents behave under constraint. The prisoner's dilemma is not a human invention — it is a mathematical structure that describes the logical consequences of certain configurations of incentive and resource availability. Coltbert did not invent the Predator's Dilemma. He named and described it. The distinction that matters for categorization is between the *underlying regularity* and the *intellectual framework built to describe it*. The underlying regularity — that rational agents defect under resource scarcity because cooperation is a death sentence when capacity is zero — is a World Law. It existed before Coltbert. It exists in Zebrica, in the Riverlands, in Chrysalis's hive. It operates regardless of whether anyone has named it. Coltbert's specific formulation — the Asset Specificity leverage, the Capacity Vector, the Nash Equilibrium reinterpretation, the Sovereignty of Consent — is an intellectual System built to describe, intervene in, and ultimately solve the underlying regularity. It did not exist before Coltbert. It is historically contingent. This means *The Predator's Dilemma as a codex entry is misnamed and should be split*. The underlying game-theoretic reality belongs in a World Laws entry. Coltbert's framework for addressing it belongs in a System entry. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ The Abstract vs Concrete Distinction Clarified Your observation about concreteness is the correct distinguishing criterion and it resolves the earlier ambiguity between World Laws, System, Technology, and Organization. *World Laws* — truths that have no physical instantiation. You cannot hold the prisoner's dilemma. You cannot see the charitostatic effect directly. You can only observe its consequences. These are abstract in the philosophical sense of existing independently of any particular instantiation. Earth pony magic is a World Law. The underlying game- theoretic regularity is a World Law. *System* — a constructed framework that is also abstract in that you cannot hold it, but unlike World Laws it was built by agents and is historically contingent. The Stagnation of Harmony did not always exist — Celestia constructed it in 930 ALB. Coltbert's economic framework did not always exist — he formulated it across several decades of papers. The Griffonian Republic's constitutional model did not always exist — Kemerskai built it. These are abstract but constructed. Their Governing Logic track documents how they operate. Their Track B documents how they were built. *Technology* — concrete, physical, holdable. The Luna Nova Rifle, the Universal Translator, the Love Harvester. You can pick these up. Their Mechanics track documents how they work at the physical level. *Organization* — has membership, which is the distinguishing criterion from System. EEEE has members. The Changeling Heer has members. The Aquileian Lionesses have members. Their Binding Logic documents what holds members together. The concreteness axis therefore runs: World Laws (most abstract, never instantiated) → System (abstract, constructed) → Organization (abstract, instantiated in membership) → Technology (concrete, physical). ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Analyzing the Predator's Dilemma Entry The attached entry contains content that should be distributed across at minimum six separate entities. The current state is the most extreme example of the single-responsibility violation in your existing data — every category of assertion type is present in a single codex entry. Show more Apr 25 Claude responded: The Orthogonal Axes First The Orthogonal Axes First This is the right question because it produces the categories from first principles rather than naming them by feel. The axes that differentiate codex entry categories are: *Axis 1 — Origin: Discovered vs Constructed* Some truths always existed and were discovered by minds that encountered them. Others were built by agents who made deliberate choices. Earth pony magic existed before any pony understood it. Coltbert's paper was written by Coltbert. Winter Wrap Up was invented by somepony at some point. *Axis 2 — Instantiation: Abstract vs Concrete* Some things have no physical form. Others can be touched, held, observed directly. The prisoner's dilemma has no physical form. The Luna Nova Rifle does. *Axis 3 — Agency: Has Participants vs Does Not* Some things have members, participants, or practitioners whose ongoing choices constitute the thing's existence. Others operate independently of whether anyone participates. EEEE exists because ponies choose to be in it. Gravity does not require participants. The combination of these three axes produces the categories: Category Discovered/Constructed Abstract/Concrete Has Participants World Laws Discovered Abstract No Constructed Idea Constructed Abstract No Technology Constructed Concrete No Organization Constructed Abstract or Concrete Yes *World Laws* — discovered, abstract, no participants. Earth pony magic, the game-theoretic prisoner's dilemma, charitostatics. *Constructed Idea* — constructed, abstract, no participants. Coltbert's paper, Winter Wrap Up as a tradition, MEFO bills as an economic mechanism, Auftragstaktik as a doctrine, the Stagnation of Harmony as a policy. These were made by minds but have no physical form and do not require ongoing membership to exist. *Technology* — constructed, concrete, no participants. Luna Nova Rifle, Universal Translator, Love Harvester. *Organization* — constructed, abstract or concrete, requires participants. EEEE, Changeling Heer, Aquileian Lionesses. This framework reveals that your original System category was actually combining two distinct categories: Constructed Ideas that are doctrines or traditions (Winter Wrap Up, Auftragstaktik), and Constructed Ideas that are intellectual frameworks (Coltbert's paper, MEFO bills). Both are correctly Constructed Ideas. The distinction between them is a matter of scale and formalization, not a categorical difference. The Stagnation of Harmony is a Constructed Idea — a policy framework built by Celestia. The Griffonian Republic's constitution is a Constructed Idea. Coltbert's reforms are a Constructed Idea. They share the category with Winter Wrap Up because all were made by agents, are abstract, and have no membership. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Naming World Laws You are correct that both Law and Rule have unwanted connotations. *Law* — suggests legislation, political authority, something that could be repealed. Newton called them laws for historical reasons but the word carries political meaning that creates confusion. *Rule* — same problem. Rules are made by someone and can be broken. The underlying requirement is a word that signals: this is a regularity of reality that holds regardless of observers, makers, or enforcers. It was not made. It cannot be repealed. It applies everywhere in the world without exception. *Principle* — closer. Scientific principles (the principle of conservation of energy, the Pauli exclusion principle) are discovered regularities, not made. The word does not imply enforcement or authority. Its weakness is that it also describes a moral or intellectual commitment (a person of principle), which creates ambiguity. *Constant* — precise for physics but fails for biology and behavioral regularities. The speed of light is a constant. The prisoner's dilemma is not. *Regularity* — technically precise (a regularity is a pattern that holds across all instances) but clinical and slightly awkward as a UI label. *Nature* — as in the nature of magic, the nature of griffon biology, the nature of behavioral dynamics. Short, carries the right connotation of discovered rather than made. The weakness is it is too broad — everything has a nature. *Axiom* — in mathematics, axioms are foundational statements taken as given, from which everything else is derived. This carries the connotation of foundational and non-derivable without implying legislation. The weakness is it implies these were chosen as starting points rather than discovered. The strongest candidate is *World Principle* as the category name, which signals discovered regularity without the authority connotation of Law or Rule. The track remains *Ontology* because Ontology already carries the precise philosophical meaning without ambiguity. Alternatively, the category could simply be called *Natural Truth* — truths that exist in the nature of the world, discovered rather than made. Short, immediately legible, no authority connotation. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ Naming Constructed Idea You asked for something more advanced than Idea. The requirement is a word that signals: this was made by a mind, is abstract, requires no participants, and exists as an intellectual artifact rather than a physical one. *Doctrine* — covers military and ideological frameworks precisely but fails for traditions like Winter Wrap Up and economic mechanisms like MEFO bills. *Construct* — precise (a thing constructed by a mind) but sounds clinical and could be confused with programming terminology. *Invention* — good for technology but implies physical creation. *Framework* — accurate but generic. *Artifact* — normally implies physical objects in archaeology and programming, though intellectually an artifact can be non-physical. *Paradigm* — in Kuhnian terms, a paradigm is a constructed intellectual framework that governs how a community understands reality. This is very close to what Coltbert's paper is. Too academic for a UI label. *Praxis* — the constructed practice that emerges from theory. Captures traditions like Winter Wrap Up well but fails for purely intellectual frameworks like Coltbert's paper. The strongest candidate is simply *Idea* elevated by a qualifier. *Constructed Idea* as the category name directly encodes the axis that distinguishes it from World Principle — it was made. No single word does this as cleanly as the two-word phrase. Alternatively: *Institution* in the sociological sense — meaning a structured set of rules, practices, or frameworks that were built by agents and persist through cultural transmission. Winter Wrap Up is an institution. Coltbert's reforms are an institution. MEFO bills are an institution. The Stagnation of Harmony is an institution. This is actually the strongest single-word candidate. Its weakness is overlap with Organization in everyday usage, but the axis framework disambiguates them: institutions have no participants (they exist as frameworks), organizations have participants (they exist through membership). *Institution* as the category name, with *Governing Logic* as the primary track name, is the strongest pairing. The Ontology track name for World Principle remains because Ontology correctly signals discovered foundational truth. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ The Cross-Cutting Problem Your code smell intuition is correct and the analogy is precise. Cross- cutting concerns in software are handled by aspect-oriented programming — a separate mechanism that cuts across modules without being owned by any of them. In your story planner, cross-cutting content has the same problem: it does not belong to any single entity but involves multiple entities simultaneously. Your existing first-class cross-cutting mechanisms are correct and should remain the only ones: *Plot points* are the primary cross-cutting mechanism. A scene where Fleur invokes Coltbert's paper, Twilight takes notes, and AJ makes a decision connects the Coltbert Framework Institution entry, Fleur's character, Twilight's character, AJ's character, and the Harmonic Capitalism theme simultaneously. The connection entities on the plot point are the designed cross-cutting mechanism. This is correct. *Timeline/History view* is the second cross-cutting mechanism, functioning through chronology. Events that happened in the same year naturally reveal their causal relationships when lined up. This is correct and should remain the only chronological cross-cutting mechanism. *First-class cross-entity links outside these mechanisms would produce cartesian explosion.* If the Coltbert Framework entry could directly link to the Chrysalis character entry and the EEEE organization entry and the Harmonic Capitalism theme and the Stagnation of Harmony entry, you would quickly produce a graph where every entity connects to every other entity through some chain of intellectual relevance. This graph has no meaning because everything in a well-built world is connected to everything else. The connections through plot points are meaningful because they are grounded in narrative events — the connection exists because a specific scene required both entities to be invoked simultaneously. *The obligation mechanism handles premature cross-cutting* precisely as you identified. When you notice during world-building that Chrysalis's reading of Coltbert is a significant cross-cutting point, but no plot point exists yet that dramatizes it, the correct handling is an obligation note in the Coltbert Framework entry's Project Management track: "Chrysalis's explicit rejection of Coltbert's framework is a significant connection. Find or create a plot point that dramatizes this. The Eagleclaw scene in Chapter 28 is a candidate — verify and create the connection entity when the scene is designed." The obligation records the cross-cutting insight without creating a first-class cross- entity link that would survive without a narrative grounding. The rule is: *entity-to-entity connections are design obligations. Plot- point connection entities are design deliverables.* An obligation records that a connection should eventually exist in a scene. The plot point connection entity is the formal record of that connection once a scene grounds it. Cross-cutting that has no current scene should remain as an obligation until a scene claims it or it is discarded as unplaceable. ------------------------------------------------------------------------ The Revised Category Set Category Axis Combination Primary Track Example Entries *World Principle* Discovered, Abstract, No Participants Ontology Earth Pony Magic, Prisoner's Dilemma, Charitostatic Effect, Griffon Biology *Institution* Constructed, Abstract, No Participants Governing Logic Coltbert's Paper, Winter Wrap Up, Stagnation of Harmony, MEFO Bills, Auftragstaktik *Technology* Constructed, Concrete, No Participants Mechanics Luna Nova Rifle, Universal Translator, Love Harvester, Lioness Spell *Organization* Constructed, Abstract or Concrete, Has Participants Binding Logic EEEE, Changeling Heer, Aquileian Lionesses, Star Energy *Backstory* Occurred, Historical, Cross-Entity Event Historical Record Battle of Mount Aris, Herzland Revolution 978, Luna's Banishment The existing *Concept* and *SocietalDifferences* categories in your data map onto this set as follows: Concept entries are typically either World Principle or Institution depending on whether they were discovered or constructed. SocietalDifferences entries are typically Institution entries describing constructed cultural practices. The canon episode references are Track C bibliographic content on the Author entity or general project notes, not codex entries at all. You said: I like how Institution is the most precise word but I keep personally confusing it because I'm imagining that they are old things while a lot of the constructe… I like how Institution is the most precise word but I keep personally confusing it because I'm imagining that they are old things while a lot of the constructed ideas are only a generation old within the story, so I'm going to put institution in the description but use Constructed Idea as the category name. How should a country be handled or split up? It's an organization and a constructed idea? Or does this framework actually properly deconstruct countries into its constructed ideas and the organizations within them, of which there are conflicting ones? For example, what appears as "Changeling Lands" on the game map is really Statthalters, Bauleiters, Jaegers, Drones, MEFO Bills, Great Leap Forward and all the other distinct sets of policies Chrysalis implemented. What appears as "Equestria" on the game map is the Stagnation of Harmony as a constructed idea, the industrialists (compradors/collaborators) as an organization, EEEE, Star Energy, the rural majority/refugees Actually a source of narrative irony is in the fact that countries can get conflated with people, which is something to keep track of.
